After 2030, even more than 50% of farms are expected to be run by people over 65 years of age. The situation is further aggravated by the low number of birthdays, which is observed in Western countries, but still in Poland. Social assistance, helping older people is mainly a task of self-government. But despite local authorities' efforts to help older people, especially the elderly, with low incomes, it still seems insufficient, still too small. Some municipalities do not carry out their own tasks at all, e.g. care services. I know municipalities that do not respond to requests at all so that no decision can be made so that this decision cannot be appealed against. I therefore ask the Minister to answer the question of what the government is doing to support the local government in carrying out its own tasks in helping older people. ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u5	Thank you very much, Member. And I would like your answer, Minister Elizabeth Bojanowska. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u6	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Government administration supports the activity of local governments through the implementation of programs, government or ministry, but also through grants to accomplish the tasks of self-governments. The most important programmes that support local authorities in the implementation of their tasks include, above all: the Senior+ programme, the 75+ programme or the multi-annual government programme, meals at school and at home, but also the Operational Programme, Food Aid and the Lifetime Programme. And now a few details. I'm gonna start with the Senior+ program. It is a program addressed to the elderly, who are still reasonably efficient, who want to be active and can leave the house. Under this programme, both municipalities and powiats receive support for the creation and maintenance of daily residence facilities for the elderly. It's about the daily residence homes and the senior+ clubs. Under this programme, local authorities in open competition can apply for funding, first, for the opening or equipment of a facility of 300 thousand. PLN in the case of a daily residence home, and in the case of a club - 150 thousand. PLN, second, to ensure the operation of existing facilities up to PLN 300 per month per person in the daily residence home and up to PLN 200 per person in the club. What do these facilities offer? Above all, these facilities offer older people, especially lonely ones, a broad catalogue of services in the field of social activation, education, sports, artistic, cultural or educational activities, but above all rehabilitation and social services, including a warm meal, as well as occupational therapy. These institutions undertake activities which aim at broadly understood activation and intergenerational integration, as regular meetings with children from kindergartens, schoolchildren or seniors from other institutions or non-governmental organisations are organised in these institutions. In total, 240 senior houses and clubs were in operation in Poland at the end of 2017, offering 6265, while in 2018 we allocated PLN 80 million. In addition, in 2018 more than 300 new facilities with more than 8200 sites are planned to be created, i.e. there is a strong increase in the number of these facilities. In the coming days there will be a call for proposals for next year. We also have a planned amount of PLN 80 million, which is why we encourage local authorities to submit proposals today. The second programme, which entered into force on 1 January 2018, which supports the elderly, the lonely, the needy and who supports local governments in carrying out their own task, is the 75+-care programme. I would like to say that in preparing for this programme, we have examined that in 2017 430 local governments did not carry out their own responsibility, namely the provision of caring services and specialist care services. In May 2018, 376 municipalities still did not carry out this task, so we launched this programme, directing it to people over 75 years of age. In 2018, the programme included single persons, in 2019 it will cover any person over 75 years of age who requires care services or specialized care services in their place of residence. In the first half of 2018, 384 municipalities joined the programme. Another programme, which I will mention, whose implementation we finance to local governments, is mainly a meal at school and at home. For this program we have the amount of PLN 550 million allocated. This programme has been under way for many years, but the structure of those who benefit from this programme is changing: the number of children and families is falling and the number of adults is increasing. In 2017, over 654 thousand benefited from this scheme. adults. In this new edition, actually in the new programme since 2019 we have directly identified a module addressed to adults who meet one of the conditions set out in the Social Assistance Act and meet the income criterion of 150% the criterion of the Social Assistance Act. Another programme, which we co-finance to local governments and which they use, among others. Older people are the Operational Programme of food aid, which is co-financed by the European Fund for Aid to the Most Deprived. Under this scheme, food aid is granted on the basis of an income criterion and here we have introduced a criterion of 200% from the Social Assistance Act so that pensioners can be in this programme as much as possible. [[♪ The bell ♪]] I would also like to mention, by not specifically discussing this, that we also have a program for life, as well as protected housing, and we also finance the implementation of the tasks of our own local authorities. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u36	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! In 1999 very important changes were made to the medical rescue system. In principle, a medical rescue system was created, which until then did not function properly in Poland. The cell of this system was intended to be an efficient system for receiving calls, i.e. the creation of medical dispatchers, which operated on the basis of certain procedures and standards. Other links were medical rescue team dispatchers, ground medical rescue teams, as well as flight rescue units. Another element of this efficient medical rescue system, which is intended to work in a life-threatening state, was the creation of hospital emergency units. In addition to hospital emergency services, trauma centers, adult trauma centers, trauma centres for children as well as specialist hospitals were created that were only involved in saving lives in the state of danger. That was the idea of medical rescue. In view of the above, a number of amendments have been made to prepare these entities for the statutory function. Hospital emergency services were provided, standards were established in these departments, including: on the employment of professionals as well as the procedures for dealing with them. So that this rescue system is dedicated to patients in a life-threatening state. The idea was for patients who are in this situation to go to these specialized centres and not to hospitals that are not prepared for it. The National Health Fund therefore contracts separate hospital emergency services, offering significantly higher rates, as well as emergency rooms that are in hospitals that are generally geared towards receiving scheduled patients. The differences in the financing of hospital emergency and emergency rooms are definitely large. We are, of course, looking at how hospital emergency services are financed on an ongoing basis, and on a national scale this is presented in various ways, but the average daily lump sum for daily readiness at national level is over 17 000. PLN, while in 2015 it was around 15 thousand. zł. This is an obvious increase, but this is the average. We have rescue teams that are financed over 38,000. zł. These are specialized units that actually take a large number of patients in life-threatening situations and carry out specialized procedures, rescue units that are funded on a scale of 7, 8 or 9 thousand. As far as the financing of reception rooms is concerned, on average in the country it amounts to about PLN 3300. There are also hospitals that have a reception room in their structure - there is much lower funding - and hospitals that have a much higher daily lump sum. As regards the financing of hospital emergency services, it is based on the so-called. the structure and function indicators. The amount of the contract depends on the number and condition of patients who are admitted to the hospital emergency department. This function indicator is valued in the form of a specific value per point. A few years ago it was 30 PLN, right now it is the amount of 52 PLN per point. So this is the system of financing these benefits. As regards the reception of patients in hospital emergency and emergency rooms, data from 2018 indicate that we adopted in emergency rooms in the first half of 2018. 1,590 480 people, and in the SORs, hospital emergency services, at the same time there were 2 320 397 people admitted, or twice as many. The network of hospital emergency services is defined in the voivodship plans, where each of the voivodships indicates where the hospital is to be a hospital emergency unit. Of course, the hospital must comply with certain standards laid down by law in order to be able to apply for such a branch and for funding. As you can see, the Minister of Health is constantly monitoring the situation. Of course, there are signals to us, as the Commissioner says, that there are very many patients in hospital emergency services in some hospitals, especially those who would not necessarily have to be in this ward. We're monitoring this situation. Until now, we have not had efficient IT systems that would allow us to accurately assess the patient's waiting time in specific cases. If for example. In medical rescue teams, we have a functioning IT system, a system to support the command of State Medical Rescue, on the basis of which we can determine exactly what time the team reaches the scene, how long the benefits are provided at the scene, how long it takes to transport to the hospital emergency department, then in the case of the SORs we are not quite able to determine that. Therefore, the Minister of Health is currently introducing pilots to assess the waiting time for admission in the hospital emergency department. [[♪ The bell ♪]] This pilot scheme will probably be put in place later this year. It covers several hospitals in Poland and on the basis of this we will draw conclusions that will apply throughout the country. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u51	You're welcome, Minister, to respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u52	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Members, assessing the student's educational achievements is about recognising the level and progress of the teacher's knowledge and skills in relation to the educational requirements arising from the basics of the curriculum at school, which takes into account the basics of the curriculum. The provisions of the Education Law do not oblige the teacher to ask the students homework, there are no such provisions, but the assessment of homework can be one of the forms of checking the teacher's educational achievements. Whether and what homework is being done to students is decided by the teacher who organizes the educational process. The aim of the homework students is to support the teaching work of the teacher, because they are a way of perpetuating or checking the messages gained during the classes, provided, of course, that the student mastered the teaching content as a result of his schoolwork and is able to do homework on his own. The fulfillment of this condition requires the teacher to ask such work, to which the student was prepared as a result of his lessons, i.e., for teaching reasons, it is not necessary, of course, to ask for tasks involving material which had not been developed before. Self-fulfilment of homework should be a way for the student and a way of perpetuating knowledge, and a way of motivation to learn. When deciding on homework, the teacher should also take into account the burden on pupils arising from the teaching of other subjects, while the Director, in the framework of the educational supervision, should monitor the compliance of teachers with the legislation on teaching, education and care activities and other statutory activities, i.e., the Director's competence is to analyse and assess how the educational process is implemented, including adjustments as regards teachers, educational requirements. Organisational arrangements for home tasks should always be implemented at school level and should be implemented at intra-school level. This will allow you to actually adjust your homework to a given educational stage, to the level of pupils and to your parents' expectations. Important actors in this matter are school councils and institutions and parents' councils operating in schools in accordance with the provisions of the Law on Education. The Board of Parents, like the school board or the institution, may address the Director with proposals and opinions on all matters relating to the functioning of the school, including requests for homework. The introduction of legislation prohibiting students from doing homework will have a significant impact on the organisation of the teaching process and will restrict the right for teachers to choose freely the methods of working with the student, and in accordance with Article. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 The Teacher Charter Act in the implementation of the curriculum has the right to apply freely such teaching methods and education as it considers the most appropriate among the recognised teachings of today. On the other hand, we will look at what the Member was talking about. The issue of homework was analysed in various international, key studies in which Poland participates, such as PISA, such as TIMSS, i.e. research on mathematical skills and mastering mathematical issues by fourth graders, or such as PIRLS, which concern reading skills of fourth graders. Such studies were also conducted by the Educational Research Institute. If we look at PISA and those that have been conducted since the beginning of this century, we will notice that in fact the number of hours spent by Polish students on homework is higher than in OECD countries. However, it is interesting to see more detailed studies of PISA in recent years, from 2015, because if we looked at them, we would see that in fact a student in total for homework and after-school tasks, because this was the last study, he spent 18.4 hours a week with us, while in OECD countries - 17.1 . In detail, a statistically 15-year-old spent 3.1 hours on natural objects in Poland, in OECD countries - 3.2 hours, on mathematics in Poland - 3.4 hours, in OECD countries - 3.8 hours, on mother tongue - 3 hours, in OECD countries - 3.1 hours. This subject, which clearly distinguishes the Polish school and Polish student from the others, is a foreign language and it simply determines these differences in hours - in Poland it is 4.9 hours, in OECD countries - 3.1 . These studies were, as I said, also carried out for the fourth graders. In the case of TIMSS, a study of math skills, as the teachers declare, the amount of homework to be done is higher than the average in OECD countries, in other words, [[♪ The bell ♪]] These tasks are actually asked more often. It is different for PIRLS, i.e. reading studies. There, this percentage is different, below the OECD average.

In contrast, there is less research on the meaning of homework and its effectiveness, in other words, the quality of the teaching process. In fact, we can try to deal with this in various ways. Looking at the Polish research carried out by the Educational Research Institute in relation to pupils in Class VI, the result of quantitative studies indicated that the student spent four to five hours a week on homework. However, it is worth noting that studies have also been conducted from a different angle. If the scope of the study were to be extended to include extracurricular activities in addition to homework, it would appear from the studies conducted in 2015 that almost 20% of the students in Class VI were involved. needs psychological and pedagogical help, and almost 40% - compensatory actions in mathematics and Polish. We also remember, of course, that there was a test at primary school earlier. Although it was not selective, but diagnostic, after class VI some students applied to junior high schools, e.g. Bilingual or other secondary schools that required additional preparation or offered a different form of recruitment. In principle, researchers wondered about the quality of all these activities, how they actually resulted from needs and how they resulted from the implementation of the Article. 42 Teacher Cards, or so-called. card hours. On the other hand, when it comes to what can be done and what should be done, it is worth paying attention to the quality of the PIRLS study I mentioned, namely the study of achievements in reading the fourth graders. That's where the quality factor was pointed out. It can be said that these studies show what appears to be quite obvious, i.e. Common sense is the most important thing. Daily homework was inefficient, non-tasking too. In other words, as the Polish teacher declared in the case of this study, homework several times a week brought the greatest teaching effect. [[♪ The bell ♪]] This was measured by the number of points. On the other hand, when it comes to supervision, it is not the Minister who supervises schools, it is the principal of the school and the head of the school - the school's curator. That way you can control what's going on at school. An important factor is also the parent's application to internal school rules that parents should be influenced by, whether by the parent's council or by the school board. In our view, this should be provided for and clearly included in school rules. Each parent and pupil should know how the tasks will be enforced and how they will be evaluated.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u75	Please, Mr. Bogdan Święczkowski, Deputy Attorney General, National Attorney, will answer that question.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u76	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! First of all, I would like to say that the Minister of Justice could not answer this question, because there is no access to such information, but, of course, such an answer can be given by the Attorney General or his first deputy, who is the national prosecutor, which is why I came here to give this answer. Of course, you must bear in mind that an intensive, advanced investigation is under way and the prosecution cannot, in detail, inform third parties of previous findings or intentions. However, in accordance with Article. 12 Laws on the Public Prosecutor's Office, given that these are important issues for the security of the State and for public policy purposes, I have decided to provide you with the information we can provide. Well, ladies and gentlemen, this investigation was initiated on 5 July 2016 in the Regional Public Prosecutor's Office in Lublin by order of sign. RP II Ds. 2.2016. It is a matter of abuse of the powers conferred on persons obliged by the Ministry of the Treasury, pursuant to the Act, to deal with property matters of the State Treasury in connection with the privatisation of the company under the company Mine Węgiel Brunatnego Adamów SA consisting of the sale of 85% shares of the company in favour of the Zespół Elektrowni Pątnów-Adamów-Konin Elektrowni Pątnów-Konin Elektrowni Pątnów-Adamów-Konin, with the acceptance for the purpose of this transaction of underestimating the value of the shares, which was caused in the name of the State Treasury by damage to property of large size, i.e. approx. PLN 90 million. This is an art crime. § 296 § 1 k.k. As the Member here mentioned earlier, this procedure was initiated by a notification by the Ministry of the Treasury of suspicion of a criminal offence based on the findings made in 2015 by the Supreme Audit Chamber in the framework of the verification of several of the largest privatisation transactions carried out by the Minister of the Treasury in 2012 and 2014. Among the controlled transactions were those sales of 85% action. In the course of this privatisation, NIK found irregularities subsequently covered by the notification. That's what I call it. It's a post-audit matter. A number of procedural actions have been carried out in the course of the two-year investigation. Very extensive evidence was collected with 46 volumes of files at the moment, over 9,000. cards. In particular, extensive documentation was obtained from the former Ministry of the Treasury, the Supreme Chamber of Control, as well as the Coal Mine of Adamów SA. The present case is also accompanied by documents drawn up by the privatisation advisor, such as reports on estimates of the value of mine shares and their updates. As witnesses of employees of the Adamów mine, the privatisation advisor of the Pątnów-Adamów-Konin Power Plant, the Supreme Audit Chamber controllers carrying out the privatisation investigation, the employees of the Ministry of Treasury carrying out activities in the context of the privatisation of the mine, as well as the former Minister of Treasury and former Deputy Minister of Treasury. A number of arrangements have been made for the parties involved in the privatisation process and the persons involved have been interviewed. A total of 51 people have been interviewed so far. Further steps are planned to provide a comprehensive explanation of all the circumstances of the case, including interrogation, in an undetermined capacity, of Mr Alexander Grad, Mr Jan Bury or Mr Zdzisław Gawlik, as well as other persons who may have knowledge of the circumstances connected with the privatisation. In the course of this investigation, it was a very important and important issue for an expert in economics and finance to obtain an opinion on the value and valuation of these shares. This opinion was obtained by experts, and in the opinion of prosecutors, this opinion is incomplete in terms of issues and privatisation processes. Therefore, the prosecutor considered that it should be supplemented after securing further evidence. Only by drawing up another opinion from experts in this field will it be possible to make a comprehensive assessment of the privatisation and to turn this procedure from the in-rem phase into an in personam phase, i.e. to present a plea for committing any crimes to the persons determined. The investigation period was extended by my decision until 20 February 2019. This case remains under the supervision of the Public Prosecutor's Office of the National Department for Economic Crime. I hope that by 23 February 2019 this procedure will, at least in part, be terminated. However, this also depends on a number of international actions, because a number of requests for international legal assistance have been made in this case. If we obtain these answers from international institutions of countries neighbouring Poland, then [[♪ The bell ♪]] this procedure may be terminated within that period. Thank you very much.

In answer to Mr Mosiński's question, I kindly inform you that the Minister of Infrastructure, together with his appointed Advisory Group for the training of persons applying to drive, has prepared a draft law amending the Act on driving vehicles. The project under preparation involves a number of changes in the system for obtaining driving licences, including: changes in the functioning of drivers' training centres aimed at eliminating market failures directly affecting the level of training for future drivers, and in particular actions related to the elimination of unfair competition. We also plan to introduce the possibility for accompanying persons to ride after completing basic training at the driver training centre. This will reduce the cost of training for drivers and allow for legalisation of the current state of affairs. We are also planning, and this is actually the main change, the transfer of responsibility for the organisation and conduct of State driving licences from the Marshal of the voivodship to the voivodship, including in particular the transfer of the financing of the organisation and the carrying out of State driving tests to the driving licence from the voivodship centres under the Marshal of the voivodship to the state budget. So these tasks will be carried out by the voivode. The fees for the exams will affect the voivodeship's account, the voivode will finance the examiners, as well as the WORDs. It is about transferring the employment of examiners from the voivodship road centre also to the voivodship. Then WORD will have no impact on the level of the state exams, and we will eliminate this pathology that the number of exams generated the profits of WORDs. This project, following the preparation by the Ministry of Infrastructure of the impact assessment of the regulation and the relevant justification, was submitted in mid-2018 to the Office of the President of the Council of Ministers to submit it to the Government's Programming Team to decide on the inclusion of this project in the list of legislative and programming work of the Council of Ministers. Right now, we're at the stage of some minor details, we're agreeing with the Center for Strategic Analysis. I hope that this will soon be included in the list of work of the Council of Ministers. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The first is to build a knowledge base on Polish exports and develop the strategic competence of managing foreign expansion of the Polish economy, the second stream is to develop the motivation and competence of entrepreneurs in the field of exports, the third stream is to support in finding a contractor, the fourth - financing and export insurance instruments, the fifth - economic diplomacy and promotion of the Polish Economy brand, the sixth - operational excellence, the seventh - communication and information, including the Portal of Export Promotion. High Izbo! Each entrepreneur obviously expects and needs a different set of information, tools and support instruments. By consulting entrepreneurs, we have identified the main problems they are facing. Companies are primarily faced with a lack of capital and human resources, fear of risks, ignorance of conditions for access to foreign markets and languages - including, of course, knowledge of the legal ecosystem of a country, especially when we are talking about countries other than the European continent - the lack of long-term planning and the need to adapt products to demand abroad. We've already launched over 50 foreign trade offices around the world. They are targeted at specific individualised support for entrepreneurs, the organisation of foreign business missions profiled for specific sectors, the implementation of support programmes dedicated to individual markets, the development of business entry strategies for foreign markets, the verification of foreign counterparties and the provision of standard services outside the country of access to information. This is the answer to all the challenges that I mentioned earlier, connected with market knowledge, with the presence in this market, so that the Polish entrepreneur is aware that there is an angel of business, who from the Polish state is on the ground and is ready to help him individually, not en block, not sectorally, not industryly, but individually approaches the needs of every entrepreneur. We are currently focusing support on key sectors, which are an important export position of the country, and those we are diagnosing, that they can play a key role in the future in improving the competitiveness of the Polish economy. The government supports 12 industries with this potential, as well as the potential to build an image, build a strong and recognizable image of the Polish Economy Brand. Programs have been developed for the following sectors: medical equipment, machinery and equipment, cosmetics, IT/ICT, biotechnology, pharmaceuticals, Polish fashion, construction and finishing of buildings, yachts and boats, furniture, Polish food specialties, health services sector as well as automotive and aviation parts. These industries are supported within the framework of the "Promotion of the economy" project based on Polish product brands - Marka Polska Gospodarki - Brand. Participation of entrepreneurs in the above mentioned programmes are also financed by European funds. We support companies primarily in entering new markets, especially Asian, African or North American, which often have significantly higher growth and trade rates than traditional EU markets for us and still have undeveloped internal demand potential. As the Ministry of Entrepreneurship and Technology, we also have four export promotion instruments, financed by budget and implemented as de minimis aid. In addition, Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego implements together with the Export Credit Insurance Corporation, KUKE, the government program Mt. Financial export support. Depending on the needs of BGK, it offers banking products to provide financing for a foreign buyer of Polish goods and services or debt financing for a Polish entrepreneur planning to expand abroad. The loans granted under the scheme are covered by KUKE insurance. The presentation during major promotional events in the world, in which we try to participate, including the most prestigious world expo exhibitions, also serves to strengthen the recognition of Polish products. Ladies and gentlemen! We approach these challenges with an attitude that Poland needs a new, fresh economic diplomacy. ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u143	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! At yesterday's meeting of the Committee on Public Finance, we considered a government bill amending the Act on Tax on Goods and Services from Print No. 2995. The project itself is a compact project, it contains only three articles, but there was quite a long discussion about this project. Two threads were raised here in a special way. The first formal thread concerned whether the bill submitted had passed through a public consultation. That's what the Members of the opposition raised. They thought that this project should go through a very broad public consultation. It was one thread. And the second topic was semantic, whether to maintain the level of the tax scale of 23% and 8% and a flat-rate tax rate on return of 7% it is to keep the tax at the same level, whether it is an increase. Since this tax scale has been in force for 8 years since 1 January 2011, maintaining it under this bill for the following years is a maintenance of the level of taxes, not an increase, and there has been quite a long dispute about it. As a result, the Minister's justification has emerged that a reduction in the tax scale would have serious implications for the budget. The draft state budget has already been presented to the High House and revenue on value added tax of about PLN 170 billion was, of course, calculated on the basis of the applicable tax scale. If the tax was theoretically reduced by 1 percentage point, not by 1%, but by 1 percentage point, which results in a tax reduction by more than 4%, exactly by 4.4% , counting the linear link between the tax scale and tax revenues, with such a linear link the loss of revenue in the state budget would amount to over PLN 7 billion. Since the government is implementing this and has introduced certain expenditures into the budget for the implementation of its programme, mainly this social policy programme, a reduction of 1 percentage point of this tax scale would make it necessary to reconstruct the draft state budget. Therefore, following the discussion, the committee voted in favour of this draft law, and on behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, I recommend that this draft law be adopted. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u232	We proceed with the examination of item 21 of the agenda: First reading of the government bill on compensation for the loss of the right to free coal and for the cessation of free coal collection by persons who are not employees of the mining company (print No 2958). I ask the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Energy of Mr Grzegorz Tobiszowski to present the justification for the bill. [[Applause]] Minister, you're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u233	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The purpose of the Government's draft law on compensation for the loss of free coal rights and for the cessation of free coal collection by persons who are not employees of a mining undertaking is to compensate, to a certain extent, within the scope of the State budget, for losses incurred by persons who are entitled not employees of mining undertakings who have received free coal in kind or in cash equivalent. These persons received free coal on the basis of collective agreements, agreements and other regulations in force in a mining undertaking which had ceased to be in force before the date of entry into force of the Act as a result of agreements concluded or terminations made. The aim is to be achieved by paying out of the budget of the State compensation for the permanent loss of the right to free coal or for the cessation of the collection of free coal by persons who are not employees of the mining company. On 24 October 2017, the Law of 12 October 2017 on compensation for the loss of the right to free coal entered into force. This law allowed for compensation to be granted to the largest group of persons who were not employees of a mining company entitled to free coal on the basis of internal labour law - pensioners and pensioners of mining companies who were unilaterally or by agreement of the parties the right to free coal. In addition, the law also included widows, widowers and orphans who had a fixed right to a survivor's pension after pensioners. The proposed regulation is the continuation of that law and at the same time the completion of the process of cleaning up the situation arising after the loss of the right to free coal by persons who were not employees of mining companies which were mining coal which was the subject of a deputation. Coal deputies have been an integral part of the life and finances of the mining community for many years. It should be recalled here that the financial situation, which at that time deteriorated in the mines, caused the rights to be removed unilaterally by the employer's termination of collective agreements guaranteeing that right or by agreement between the parties to those agreements, i.e. the social side and the employer. During the application of the Act of 12 October 2017 on compensation in the mining environment, there were voices that, in addition to pensioners, pensioners, widows and orphans after these persons, other groups of persons also lost their right to free coal and, under the principle of equality, should also be granted compensation for the loss of that right. After gathering experience in the functioning of the Act, numerous social signals and information from the affected entrepreneurs, enabling the identification of other categories of persons, a catalogue of persons entitled to compensation covered by this regulation was created. According to the draft law, persons entitled are among others. entitled to free coal on the basis of collective agreements, agreements or other regulations in force in a mining undertaking which have ceased to apply before the date of entry into force of the Act, as a result of agreements concluded, and so: former workers whose employment relationship has been terminated with a mining undertaking under the Act of 13 March on special arrangements for the termination of their employment relations with workers, widows, widows and orphans having a fixed right to a survivor's pension after a deceased in the course of the employment relationship, pensioner or pensioner from a mining undertaking, if the loss of entitlement to free coal has not been compensated to them in any other way, widows, widows and orphans receiving the special benefits referred to in Article. 82 Act on Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund, either having a fixed right to a survivor's pension after a worker of a mining undertaking who died during a working relationship or a former employee who acquired the right to a pre-retirement benefit, collecting free coal in kind or in cash equivalent, to which the mining undertaking ceased to spend free coal or to pay a cash equivalent before the date of entry into force of this Act. It is necessary to clarify the fact that in the draft law the entitled widowers, widowers and orphans who are entitled to a survivor's pension are included in the catalogue of entitled widows, widowers and orphans who are entitled to a survivor's pension after the deceased's employees during their employment relationship and after pensioners if the loss of that entitlement has not been compensated in any other way. The purpose of the regulation that we are currently proposing is to align the status of these persons with that of other persons who have remained in the same situation and who have never been compensated for the loss of this entitlement due to differences in the payment of benefits by companies. This is a kind of supplement and at the same time fill the gap in the system that has been shaken in previous years. Following the introduction of the 2017 Act and the current Act, we supplement and complete the system in respect of persons who have been deprived of the right to a coal depot in nature or to a paid deputy. In order to implement the compensation payment, the Act designates the paying companies. It will be the Company of Restructuring of Kopalń SA, which on behalf of the Coal Company, Katowice Holding of Coal, KWK Kazimierz Juliusz will pay, collect applications from those persons who, in respect of these establishments, had their powers and were assigned to mines within this structure. Then there will be people associated with the Jastrzębska Coal Company, Tauron Extraction and Lublin Coal Bogdanka. This division has been made because companies are actively in possession of data and are staffed for this purpose. Our purpose is to carry out the whole procedure as we would like, as before. This is actually the last piece of legislation that will allow us to close this cycle. The holder shall be required to submit an application within 90 days of the entry into force of the Act. The important information is that on June 1st we start payouts. I think that as far as this bill is concerned, we should close the process this year, so at the beginning of next year we would start accepting applications. Of course, this will also be preceded by an information campaign. As before, I will also address the parliamentary offices, because it is a good habit that representatives of all the communities represented in Parliament join together in the promotion of this law and at the same time in its smooth implementation. This was then a very large number of people, because more than 200,000, today according to our estimates and according to the analysis of all the documents I mentioned, we are talking about 24 000. persons. It is also important that, in addition to the possibility for widows and orphans to exercise this right, we have added the possibility of exercising this right by persons who have either failed to submit an application under the 2017 Act, or for whom companies - and we have such cases - have failed to comply with the relevant formalities due to a certain circulation of documents, and the payment resulting from the Act has been suspended, but not because of the applicant's fault. Here we create an opportunity for all groups. In this way, those who have not made an application to the SRK will have to submit an application and, in the case of those who have submitted the application which has been examined, the documents will have to be completed and the application will be re-examined without being submitted to the institution which will be responsible for verifying the applications and for preparing the relevant payment decisions. As far as the most important financial impact is concerned, because this is important, the compensation amounts to 10,000. zł. The amount of compensation shall not be equivalent to the coal available for years in which it was no longer issued to entitled persons or for future years in which it could be issued. That's why we call it compensation. Secondly, as I mentioned, the implementation will be from 1 June. In the bill we enter a whole group of people who are entitled to do this, i.e. 24 080 people, because that's what this group counts, according to our calculations, and this is a closed collection. The limit of expenditure from the State budget for financing one-off compensation in 2019 is set out in the Law at the level of 240,8 million. The compensation will be paid within the limits of mining expenditure. This cost was calculated on the assumption that, as previously, all eligible applicants would submit appropriate applications. Let me just remind you, that's when it happened, like, $240,000. persons entitled to submit applications Little more than 200,000, the rest of the funds were returned to the budget after the entire programme had been cleared. Of course, the law is accompanied by appropriate proposals - these proposals are at the same level of detail as in the case of the previous law - and a declaration that the person who wishes to exercise the right will not appear in court with claims and, at the same time, if he is in dispute in court, he must provide such information. If she wants to apply again in court, she will not be able to exercise the right under this law. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I'm making this bill that we talked about here. Let me just add that a few months, required less than six months was connected, among others. With the collection of documents... As you can imagine, those who have known the transformation process since 1989 have been a turbulent and very dynamic process: first the independent operation of mines, then coal companies, then the establishment of the Coal Company, the holding company. For this reason, we have made a really big effort, for which I thank all the employees of the Ministry, but also all the employees of the Company for Restructuring of Mines, all the employees in companies that have posted persons to reach the archives, to all these agreements and at the same time to balance the persons that may be covered by this law. We are aware that this law closes a cycle and at the same time builds a system to compensate those who have been deprived of their rights. Thank you, Marshal. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u234	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. As the first Mr Grzegorz Matusiak on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u235	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! Minister! Colleagues, fellow Parliamentarians! The hard coal mining sector is one of the most important in the Polish economy, so solving problems in this sector is important for the state. In recent years, particularly coal mining has undergone a major change after many years of neglect, and the current government's restructuring programme for hard coal has helped to halt the tragic situation in this sector and has halted the collapse of mining into the abyss. For nearly 3 years, the change in the approach to coal mining has been about profound organisational and legal changes, and we can now see growth in this sector. As we all know, problems in implementing the right to free coal began a few years ago when, due to the deteriorating financial situation, mining companies began to express their right to the coal debt to pensioners, rencistoms and other non-employees. These measures have, in fact, improved the financial situation of mining companies, mainly on an ad hoc basis, but at the same time have contributed to reducing mining privileges for non-employees, inter alia. widows from deceased workers of mining companies. We've all seen what happened between 2012 and 2015. It was the previous government, the PO-PSL government, that allowed people who were not employees to pay their mining benefits, among others. to pensioners and workers' widows, although these benefits have been an integral part of the life and finances of the mining community for many years. On 24 October 2017, the Law of 12 October 2017 on compensation for the loss of the right to free coal on the basis of which the compensation was paid to pensioners and widows, widows and orphans having a fixed right to a survivor's pension after pensioners who retired or retired from mining companies operating on 31 December 2011 on the basis of a concession. On the basis of this law, over 211 thousand compensations were received from the State budget. persons with a total amount of over PLN 2 billion. The government's draft act on compensation for the loss of free coal rights and for the cessation of free coal collection by persons who are not employees of a mining company is the next and last step in the process of settling the situation arising after the loss of free coal rights by persons who are not employees of mining companies which were mining coal which was the subject of the deputy. The project was covered by persons, who, like pensioners, had their right to the coal depot terminated by coal companies. These include, above all, widows, widowers and orphans of deceased workers, those with pre-retirement benefits, as well as widows with special pensions. The project was also covered by entitled persons who did not receive compensation under the Law of 12 October 2017 for various reasons. This latter social group has been restored, as it were, the possibility of receiving compensation, in particular because of the fact that this was the case for older people. This is the way out to meet them, so that they too will receive the compensation they deserve. The coal depot, i.e. the entitlement to receive a certain amount of free coal per year, is a very old mining power traditionally granted to both workers and pensioners, mining rencists and other non-employees, granted by a mining undertaking on the basis of internal labour law. The proposed law will cover a group of around 24 thousand. persons who, on the basis of the proposed law, will receive a one-off compensation of 10 000. zł. In total, this is to cost the state budget more than PLN 240 million. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the solution presented in print No 2958 aimed at ending the process of cleaning up the situation of persons entitled to free coal and not employees of mining companies, and calls on the High Sejm to urgently adopt a government bill on compensation for the loss of the right to free coal and for the cessation of collection [[♪ The bell ♪]] free coal by persons who are not employees of a mining company. Thank you very much in this place...

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u237	...a draft of this law, as well as Mrs. Maria Przystanska, who supported us substantively. She's a miner's widow. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u273	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a draft law amending certain laws in order to improve the effectiveness of the enforcement of maintenance services, which is the implementation of the so-called. maintenance package contained in the document adopted by the Council of Ministers on 4 April 2017 A review of family support schemes. Parents' commitment to their children is fundamental and impossible release them from this obligation. It is the parents who, in accordance with the provisions of the Family and Care Code, have the right and obligation to take care of the child, and it is the parents who have the primary responsibility to provide the child with the means of subsistence. This obligation applies equally to both parents, whether they live together or separated and regardless of which parent the child lives with. It is unacceptable that one parent or both parents shirks their obligation to put on a child, explaining that they do not live with a child or have difficulty finding a job. A situation in which one parent fails to fulfil his obligation to maintain a child results in the child's need being hindered. A maintenance debtor who does not pay alimony deprives his child of prospects, deprives him of his opportunity to develop and puts him in a worse position at the start than children from families who are not affected by the problem of non-alimentation. At the same time, the situation on the labour market has improved and unemployment is falling and it is increasingly easier for workers to find legal jobs that match their qualifications. In addition, steady wage increases are recorded. Therefore, it is difficult to find any excuse for parents who claim that they cannot find legal employment while actually working in black and earning illegal income and thus throwing the entire burden of supporting the child into a second parent. It should be pointed out that although the collection of the State budget's claims on the maintenance fund's benefits paid over the last 3 years has increased by nearly 100% , of less than 13% in 2015 to 33% in 2018, is its level it should still be considered unsatisfactory. The total amount of unenforced maintenance debtors' obligations to the Treasury arising from the payment from the State budget of benefits in replacement of unpaid maintenance is still very high. The amount of debt only for liabilities to the new maintenance fund at the end of 2017 amounted to more than PLN 10 300 million. Therefore, in order to ensure a further increase in the collection of child support, as well as in the State's budget due to the State's budget for the replacement of unpaid maintenance benefits, it is necessary to make further changes. The draft law includes, in particular, arrangements for improving the flow of information between the relevant services and institutions and judicial bailiffs on maintenance debtors, including information on their taking up employment. Its aim is to introduce tools to strengthen the effectiveness of the enforcement of maintenance services. The proposed law introduces solutions for the smooth obtaining of information by bailiffs, as well as their constant updating, on the income of maintenance debtors on the basis of the Social Insurance Institution's data. The bailiffs will obtain information from the Social Insurance Institution by electronic means, while the Social Security Office will update data on the maintenance debtor at least once a month in case of changes in the insured account. This will allow the bailiff to act quickly against the maintenance debtor in the event of legal work being undertaken by him each time. As regards the amendments to the Implementing Penal Code, they consist in making available the State Labour Inspection data on the whereabouts of maintenance debtors subject to a penalty for restricting freedom in the form of electronic surveillance. Other changes are also made that are described in the explanatory memorandum. As regards sanctions, it is proposed to extend the material responsibility of dishonest employers by introducing changes to the Labour Code. In the case of employment by an employer of a person in the National Debt Register because of a breach of maintenance obligations without written confirmation of an employment contract with the employee, and when the employer is unfairly paying to the person in the National Debt Register because of a breach of maintenance obligations, a remuneration higher than that resulting from the contract concluded without deduction of maintenance benefits may be imposed on the employer in the amount of PLN 1500 to 45 thousand. zł. It is also proposed to amend the Civil Procedure Code by increasing the amount of the fine to 5 000. PLN for an employer who has not fulfilled the obligations relating to the occupation of the maintenance debtor by the bailiff. At the same time, it is envisaged to increase the amount of the fine to which the court is competent up to 15 000. zł. It is also proposed to amend the Employment Promotion Act and the Labour Market Institutions, according to which the organiser of public works will be required to employ maintenance debtors first. This means that one of the conditions for such employment will be to hold alimony debts and guarantee the right to employ such persons. The project also envisages a change in the income criterion that entitles you to benefits from the maintenance fund. The maintenance fund benefits will be granted if the income of the family per person does not exceed PLN 800, which should increase the number of children benefiting from these benefits by around 60,000. It is proposed that the increase in the income criterion should apply from the eligibility period starting on 1 October 2019. The impact on the state budget is PLN 77 million in 2019 and about PLN 260 million in 2020. These are the main changes we propose. We assume that the Act will enter into force on 1 January 2019. As regards the amendments to the Labour Code, they will enter into force on 1 January 2020. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I ask the High House to accept the proposed amendment to the law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u315	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The project submitted aims, firstly, to eliminate unauthorised interference in the condition of the counter, to reverse it, secondly, to increase the certainty of consumer turnover, to avoid such a situation, because those who often devote their life's work put in place many years to acquire the desired four wheels, unfortunately fall into the hands of fraudsters and find those who use this type of unfair practice in order to obtain the property benefits. According to the statistics of the European Union, up to 20% of cars in the country have reversed, twisted counters. When it comes to cars crossing borders, the data is even more alarming: every other car, about 50% cars crossing the border have recuperated counters, while information from the German area, which reaches us, shows that almost 90% Cars imported from Germany may have recuperated counters. So this is a huge social problem, a huge economic problem for all those who purchase used cars. This change is therefore expected, proposed and finally becomes a reality. The project involves two very important changes. The first, as regards the criminal element, introduces a new offence, a new type of prohibited act consisting, first, of an unauthorized change in the indication of the motor vehicle's speedometer, secondly, of an unauthorised interference in the correctness of the measurement of that trafficometer, thirdly, on the order of such activities to another person. With regard to new infringements, we anticipate that unauthorised replacement of the road-meter will be a breach and failure to comply with the obligations relating to the interchange procedure will also be a violation. Therefore, these criminal provisions will provide for a sentence of between 3 months and 5 years respectively, and in the case of minor acts they will also be punishable by other penalties, such as a sentence of limitation of liberty or a fine. In the event of an offence, a fine of up to 3 000 will be imposed. Or punishment. The second element of the proposed amendment is the new administrative obligations imposed on vehicle owners, vehicle owners and relevant State services. What will they depend on? First, the owner or holder of a vehicle, if it replaces the counter with a new one, shall be required to present it within 10 days of its replacement at any vehicle inspection station - either a base station or a district station - in order to read the indication of the newly mentioned meter together with the unit of measurement by the authorised diagnostic. That's one thing. Secondly, he will be required to make a declaration on the exchange of the traveler under penalty of criminal liability for false testimony. That's two. Another obligation imposed on the owner or holder of a vehicle will be that the replacement of the road-meter will only be possible in two cases: first, where such a road-meter does not measure the vehicle's course properly in the situation in which it should be done and secondly, where it is necessary to replace the vehicle component with which the road-meter is inextricably linked. The replacement of the tracker, that is obvious, can only take place on a tracker that is efficient and appropriate for this type of vehicle. Duty imposed on the service. Police, Border Guard, Road Transport Inspection, Military Police and Customs Department receive new read-out rights in each controlled vehicle and transfer it to the central vehicle register during each roadside inspection. Whether the vehicle is registered in Poland or abroad, whether it is unregistered, whether it moves alone, or e.g. is imprisoned on a lava or towed, also such privileges indicated here will be granted to the services. As regards these issues, I would like to point out that the portal run by the Ministry of Digitalization www. The vehicle's history. gov.pl, which already functions as a free report implemented from the central vehicle register, will gain a new quality, because this information, which we are talking about here, after each exchange, will be transmitted to this portal. As a result of these changes, the portal will also contain data on the indication of the vehicle tracker read during the inspection by these services and diagnostics, regardless of whether the vehicle is registered in Poland or not. So there could be a situation where a German citizen is staying, for example. in Poland on holidays, during routine road inspection the state of the counter will be checked and will already be visible on this portal by the central register of vehicles. If he sells such a vehicle in the future, he will have a clear story behind him. The project was subject to inter-ministerial agreements, opinions and public consultations. Comments were received on the project submitted by a number of resorts, all of which were taken into account. The project was adopted by the Permanent Committee of the Council of Ministers and then by the Council of Ministers, taking into account the comments of the Governmental Legislative Centre. Therefore, I kindly ask you to accept it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u347	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Council of Ministers adopted a draft law amending the Law on Road Traffic and certain other laws submitted by the Minister of Infrastructure. The project involves changes to the vehicle technical testing system. The solutions adopted should improve road safety and environmental protection. The new rules also adapt Polish law to solutions adopted in the European Union. According to the project, the organisation, operation and supervision of high-quality vehicle technical testing systems, as well as operators operating vehicle control stations and diagnostic training centres will be responsible for Transport Technical Supervision. It is a subsidiary of the Minister of Infrastructure. Until now, the supervision of the operators operating the vehicle control stations was up to 380 starosts. However, the Supreme Audit Chamber has negatively assessed the performance of some of them. In addition, the survey carried out by the Minister of Infrastructure on new tasks resulting from the implementation of Directive 2014/45/EU has provided information that old age may not be able to carry out tasks imposed on Poland by Union legislation. In turn, diagnostic training centres operate on a free market basis, but without any supervision. This results in poor preparation of candidates for diagnostics and translates into low passability of exams. The project therefore provides for a coordinated system for the training of diagnostics working at vehicle control stations. These are specific requirements for training centres - infrastructure plus lecturers - and efficient supervision of them. A system will also be set up for conducting appropriate examinations and compulsory training for both initial and cyclical diagnostics reminding and expanding knowledge about vehicle design and the way in which they are tested. The Director of Transport Technical Supervision has been entrusted with the supervision of the diagnostic training system and the operation of diagnostic training centres. The new requirements are a consequence of the guidelines laid down in European Union legislation. The Director of Transport Technical Supervision has also been appointed to maintain the National Contact Point responsible for the exchange of information related to technical testing of vehicles with the other countries of the European Union and the European Commission. The solutions proposed in the project aim to improve road safety and environmental protection by sealing technical testing systems; to improve the quality of diagnostic work, the level of their qualifications and competence and to improve the reliability of their technical tests through the introduction of mandatory periodic trainings for them; to improve the level of road safety and the safety of the trading of vehicles on the secondary market by subject to special supervision of the verification and control of the state of the vehicle counter of the vehicle control stations and the registration of CEPiK 2.0 ; increased availability of data for citizens and entrepreneurs through the introduction of a vehicle inspection station register by the TDT Director, currently operates 380 vehicle control station registers; increased quality and reliability of tests by requiring periodic checking of calibration and maintenance of control and measurement equipment and archiving of test results; improved environmental status by reducing the average level of emissions resulting from the detection and elimination of vehicles with malfunctioning emission control systems and the elimination of leaks causing leaks in toxic liquids, leaks in gas installations as well as causing excessive noise; changes in vehicle control stations which will affect the higher quality of the technical tests carried out and involve the elimination from road traffic of vehicles in poor technical condition, which should increase the level of road safety. In order to meet the expectations of operators operating vehicle control stations, it is envisaged, among others. reducing or abolishing the fees they incur while maintaining the current toll rates charged to drivers for the technical tests of the vehicle. At the same time, in connection with the implementation of EU legislation, operators operating vehicle control stations will be responsible for the so-called. a qualitative fee intended to improve the operation of the vehicle technical testing system. The final amount of the qualitative fee of not more than PLN 3.5 will be set out in the implementing act, namely the Minister of Infrastructure Regulation, and will apply from 2020. The amount of the qualitative fee will depend on the amount of revenue generated by the fee for technical studies carried out after the deadline, namely the 45-day deadline, since it is assumed that the main source of financing of the system for ensuring high quality technical tests will be the fee for technical studies carried out after the deadline. The revised solutions are to apply 3 months after the date of publication in the Official Journal, except for parts of the rules that will apply in other time limits. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u348	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Aleksander Forczynski, Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u349	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour on behalf of the club Law and Justice to present a position regarding the draft law amending the Law on Road Traffic and certain other laws, print No 2985. The project concerns the creation of a coherent system for the supervision of technical testing of vehicles and the operation of vehicle control stations to ensure a high level of technical testing and to improve road safety. The project under investigation is intended to allow for a smooth response in the event of irregularities, to eliminate them and to prevent and operate irregularities related to the conduct of technical tests. The EU Directive 2014/45/EU, as in the previous 2009 Directive, sets out the minimum requirements for the technical testing of vehicles necessary for the introduction by individual Member States of the Union, creating a framework for the establishment of a proper and efficient system for the operation of technical tests on vehicles. At the same time, this Directive gives Member States the opportunity to tighten up their requirements due to the specificities of their national infrastructure and the structure of their means of transport, resulting in national differences, inter alia. concerning the scope and frequency of the technical tests. This law also takes into account technical developments in the field of automotive and once again regulates all issues relating to the system of technical testing. In order to implement the Union Directive, the proposed solutions provide for the establishment of a coherent system for the supervision of vehicle technical tests and the operation of vehicle control stations to ensure a high level of technical tests or, I repeat, improvement of road safety and the prevention and elimination of technical tests. In view of the fact that the system of technical tests in Poland requires a thorough conversion, this law contains provisions which go beyond the requirements of the EU Directive. Solutions outside the scope of the Directive include, inter alia, the following:: introducing, clarifying or updating definitions of concepts such as: the tram, trailer, semi-trailer or bus, as well as the provision relating to the technical tests of agricultural tractors and trailers in infrastructure other than the vehicle control station, technical tests or approval tests. The most important changes made by this project are due to the obligation to implement the Directive. The implementation of this EU-level act aims to achieve the basic objective set by the European Commission, namely the zero vision that EU countries should reduce the number of fatalities in road transport to almost zero by 2050. The vehicle technical testing system is part of a wider system to ensure that vehicles are kept at a safe and acceptable level from the point of view of the user. It should be pointed out that, in view of the new concept of a system for ensuring high-quality technical research, the Director of Transport Technical Supervision has been given a new opportunity - to spend funds to cover the costs of development work to improve road safety, including the technical state of vehicles. The indication of this possibility is dictated by the continuous development of technology, especially of the automotive industry. The creation of a central surveillance of technical testing of vehicles in the form of a TDT-separated surveillance cell is included here due to the organisational bases. The existing regulations for the supervision of vehicle control stations have been substantially amended, and these changes are intended to ensure, as I said, an improvement in safety. The draft also sets out the existing provisions for obtaining diagnostic powers and the circumstances relating to the exercise and monitoring of the profession itself. In view of the obligations imposed by the Directive, the TDT Director will exercise control over the diagnostic environment and the legality of their decisions. Minister! Dear Marshal! High Izbo! In view of the above position, the Law and Justice Club supports the Government's draft Print Act No 2985. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
