I ask Mrs Monica Rosa to present the reasons for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u385	Mrs. Marshalkini! High Izbo! The aim of the project is to introduce stricter sanctions for drivers than is currently the case for moving cars which have either a faulty or cut-out particulate filter, and for those entities which offer such removal or modification services to the particulate filter. In Poland it is common to move cars that have either cut out or used DPF filters. This practice is very harmful to the environment, human health and life, but it is unfortunately widespread. This is mainly about cars that are imported, used. There are now huge numbers of such cars coming to Poland from countries mostly Western Europe. A very large part of these imported cars are powered by diesel engines. Only between 2016 and 2017, around 2 million cars were imported to Poland, which are largely over 10 years old. The filters we are talking about, namely particulate filters, DPF filters, are mounted in the exhaust systems of diesel engines and petrol engines. They purify the exhaust gases from the soot and ash particles. An engine that works without such a filter generates significant amounts of pollution, which contributes to reducing air quality, especially in cities where car traffic is very intense. Certainly not only did I feel it, but you also had the opportunity to feel it, breathing into your lungs the air especially in big cities, in the streets, how much such cars pollute this air. Filters in such cars should be treated with maintenance procedures, they must be replaced to ensure proper operation and that pollutants in the air are as low as possible. But that's not what's happening. The presence of the filter reduces the performance of the car, and the installation of the new one is of course a high cost, reaching 7 zł. Due to these high costs, these filters are very often removed, they are replaced by fakes and installations to fool the software of imported cars. Although removing the filter leads to the fact that the vehicle becomes legally unfit for use, this practice is not sufficiently prosecuted or punished. Let us give an example of the amount of the mandate that is in force for moving a vehicle that does not comply with the emission standards. This is a maximum of about 500 PLN, so they are disproportionately small in relation to the savings that car owners generate after removing such a filter, or to the costs they are to spend when they want to change such a filter. A car like this, ladies and gentlemen, shouldn't be on the road. However, removing it from the road after removing such a filter is not mandatory. The police are obliged to keep the registration card and issue a ticket after the inspection. For example, in Austria, the penalty for removing such a filter and moving disabled under the law is a mandate of up to 3.5 thousand. Euros and car tow at the owner's expense. In the Czech Republic, the mandate is about 50,000. crowns, that's about 8,000. PLN, in Germany, the fines range from 1500 to 2500 thousand. Euros. The project that we have submitted and which we are presenting today provides for much stricter sanctions for the infringement of removing the DPF filter. First, the owner, the holder, the user of the vehicle, whose technical condition violates environmental requirements, and the person driving such a vehicle will be liable to fine up to 5 000. zł. Secondly, the same fine will threaten those who ultimately remove the particulate filter and offer a removal service. The design imposes an obligation to remove the vehicle from the road at the expense of the owner if it is found that the technical condition of the vehicle is in breach of environmental requirements. What will be the positive effects of the proposed law? First of all, social and environmental. Improving air quality, on which, I understand, we care about everyone who sits here, regardless of the political option, will be the result of a more effective fight against the removal of particulate filters. This will reduce the costs that generate health problems caused by air pollution. These are things that can easily be converted into money, but health is priceless. The higher fines for those benefiting from the removal of DPF filters will constitute additional State income. The project will not be an additional burden on the state budget, severe sanctions can be imposed under the current control system. Ladies and gentlemen! Once again, we are talking about the health and life of citizens. We are talking about the fact that it is not possible to move disabled cars on Polish roads. You also talked about the previous draft law on the fact that the bad technical condition of cars leads very often to accidents. In the case of this draft law, it must be said that the poor technical condition of cars or the deliberate removal of DPF filters contribute to an increased health risk. There can be no consent for Polish law to allow us to say: okay, you can import cars, okay, you can remove the DPF filter, you can move a disabled car, because only 500 PLN of fine is at risk for this, because disabled cars are to a large extent the cause of the reduction in air quality, which we all breathe. Therefore, I ask you to support this bill. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u386	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Waldemar Buda, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u387	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the Code of Offences and the Law on Road Traffic. The proposed act shall, as shown, increase the penalties for acts and the acts of cutting or deactivating particulate filters in vehicles, and the powers to possible towing of vehicles shall be introduced in the event of such an act of limiting the operation of such a vehicle component or cutting it out. Ladies and gentlemen! Of course, the diagnosis of the problem is correct, because it actually exists, and its scale, it can be said, is growing, but in particular not because more and more vehicles are being imported, because, as you yourself have pointed out in the explanatory memorandum, vehicles that are more than 10 years old, and these are imported from abroad, have not yet had this element. This is an element that came up seven, eight years ago and it's in new vehicles. Of course, these situations happen, but not in connection with the import of old cars that are over 10 years old. But the question is: What way should we go if we want to solve this problem? The roads are basically two: one is an increase in penalties for drivers, as proposed in this project, i.e. increasing liability in the rules of the Code of Offences, as in this case from a maximum of 500 to 5 000. zł and the second, which is already designed and presented in the project that we discussed earlier, is to bring about a situation in which this component of vehicle performance will be tested in the framework of technical tests. In our assessment, inclusion of the exhaust quality test element also within the scope, you can say, the ecology of the vehicle, can be analysed at the stage of the periodic technical examination. Therefore, in our view, it is important to go in the direction proposed by the Ministry: the examination of this element by the diagnostics. Why? Because today we know that even an increase in penalties for such incidents is problematic because such offences are not detectable, i.e. The police are not prepared to analyze this situation on the road. It is difficult to imagine that any officer controlling vehicles on the road would have quite complicated apparatus, because it would generate incredible costs from the point of view of the police, while all diagnostic stations can be and often are already equipped with equipment that allows to test exhaust gases and their quality. For these reasons, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will oppose this bill and ask for its rejection. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u388	Thank you. Please take the floor of Mr Arkadiusz Myrch, the Civic Platform.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u389	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, I would like to present my position on the draft law contained in Seym Printing 2699, which aims to identify two new offences. Firstly, the proposal provides for stricter sanctions, i.e. Fines up to 5,000. zł for drivers for moving cars with a defective or cut-out particulate filter and for operators who offer services for cutting or modification of a particulate filter. In addition, as the designers themselves stress, this is an attempt to meet the demands made by the Institute of Automotive Transport in an expert opinion of the "Procedure" to detect the removal of a particulate filter from the vehicle. This parliamentary initiative, of course, must, in principle, be regarded as very positive not only in the context of the fight for a healthy environment, which, of course, is becoming increasingly important year by year, but above all, to combat the practice which aims to intentionally remove these filters from cars, from motor vehicles. Therefore, here I will allow myself to disagree with my previous speaker representing the Law and Justice Club, who said that this control should be limited only to the technical examination of the car, which takes place once a year. This is not sufficient. It is necessary to detect this at every stage, and it is our duty as parliamentarians to prepare not only legal bases, but also other standards that would enable officers or other relevant services to implement such rules. Therefore, in addition to the introduction of the tightening of the criminal penalty, the proposal also requires that the fine threatens also those offering and performing services to cut out a particulate filter or to make any other modification thereof which would result in a breach of environmental protection. This complements what I just said so as not to restrict this type of control only to technical tests, because it would actually be limited to liability by the owner or owner of a vehicle who would report this vehicle to the technical condition test. Therefore, the Civic Platform Club assesses this project in a positive way, including a proposal which imposes an obligation to remove the vehicle from the road at the expense of the owner if it is found that its technical condition is in breach of environmental protection requirements. Legal observations were submitted to the bill by the President of the Public Prosecutor General of the Republic of Poland and by the Supreme Court. However, in the assessment of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, these suggestions should be analysed. Therefore, when assessing, in principle, the intentions of the designers and the design of the project itself, we ask that it be directed to further work in the committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

[[Applause]] [[Voice from the room: Minister Jurgiel...]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u509	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I have the pleasure and the honour to present to you a draft law on the protection of personal data processed in connection with the prevention and fight against crime. The provisions of the proposed law will be implemented by the Member States in Article. 63 Directive 2016/680 of 27 April 2016 on the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data by competent authorities for the purposes of the prevention, investigation, detection and prosecution of criminal offences and the enforcement of penalties, on the free movement of such data and repealing Council Framework Decision 2008/977/JHA, the obligation to transpose its provisions into the national legal order. Directive 2016/680, known as the Police Directive, and key to the new concept of personal data protection Regulation No 2016/679 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016 on the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data and on the free movement of such data and repealing Directive 95/46/EC in a comprehensive and consistent manner for the European Union as a whole govern data protection issues. The European legislator deliberately excluded from the scope of Regulation 2016/679 the processing of personal data by competent authorities for the purposes of crime prevention, investigation, detection and prosecution of criminal offences or the enforcement of penalties, including protection against and the prevention of threats to public security, by regulating these matters, because of the particular nature of such activities, in a different legal act, namely Directive 2016/680. The Directive, as a legal act requiring Member States to establish a given legal order - unlike a regulation which is directly applicable - allows for the diversity and diversity of national legislation on the prevention and fight against crime to be taken into account in its provisions. An example of this kind of difference is the distinction made by the Polish legislator between criminal offences and offences, the source of which is the assessment of the harmfulness of social acts, which consequently translates into the differentiation of severity of sanctions for crimes and offences. Such a system of criminal offences is not common in the European Union. An important reason for this was, on the one hand, the need to ensure a consistent, high level of protection of personal data of individuals, and, on the other hand, to facilitate the exchange of personal data between the competent authorities of the Member States so as to ensure effective cooperation in criminal matters and police cooperation, and to ensure that data can be transferred to a third country, provided that the purpose of such action is to prosecute criminal offences while ensuring an adequate level of data protection by the third country. At the same time, the Directive also includes any other authority or entity to which the law of a Member State entrusts the exercise of public authority and the exercise of public powers for the purposes set out in Directive 2016/680, in addition to public authorities such as the Police or other law enforcement authorities. It also regulates the exchange of information covering personal data between law enforcement authorities of the Member States of the European Union and third countries. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, In Polish legal realities, personal data protection issues are regulated in the Personal Data Protection Act of 29 August 1997 and in a number of other laws governing the operation of law enforcement and justice authorities, as well as other entities operating in the area defined by the framework of the subject-matter scope of Directive 2016/680. One of the main objectives of the new regulation is to maintain a balance between the right to privacy and the need for the police to maintain a balance between the right to privacy and the need for police - and other actors in the area of preventing and combating crime, including protection against and the prevention of threats to security and public order - confidentiality in the processing of data by competent authorities in this field. Regulation allowing the processing of personal data by public authorities and restricting the individual powers of the bodies referred to in Article. 51 Constitutions should find a clear and precise basis in the Act. It is not only a formal provision that gives public authorities the power to process personal data, but also the appropriate quality of those provisions. They should not confer excessive decision-making powers on public authorities in this area - e.g. by using indiscriminate terms or general clauses - and, where such freedom is necessary, there should be an effective appeal mechanism to enable the legality of the actions undertaken to be effectively monitored by independent authorities, in particular courts. In view of the above, this draft law, which sets out the permissible purposes for the processing of personal data within the meaning of the Act, and, first, the principles and conditions for the protection of personal data processed by the competent authorities for the purposes of the recognition, prevention, detection and control of criminal offences, including threats to security and public order, as well as for the execution of temporary arrests, penalties and criminal penalties and coercive measures resulting in deprivation of liberty; secondly, the rights of persons whose personal data are processed by the competent authorities for the purposes indicated and the legal protection of those persons; thirdly, the way in which the protection of personal data processed by the competent authorities for the above purposes is carried out; fourthly, the tasks of the supervisory authority - the President of the Office for the Protection of Personal Data - and the form and manner in which they are carried out; fifthly, the duties of the controller and the processor and of the data protection officer and the procedure for their designation; sixthly, the means of safeguarding personal data; seventhly, the procedure for cooperation with the supervisory authorities of other Member States of the European Union; eighth, criminal liability for breaching the provisions of that law. High Seym! A necessary element of the proposed solutions is the precise definition of the scope of this law. Personal data within the meaning of the proposed rules may be processed for the purposes of the detection, detection and eradication of and prevention of criminal offences, including threats to security and public order, as well as the execution of provisional arrests, penalties, criminal penalties and coercive measures resulting in deprivation of liberty. The fight against criminal offences should also be understood to include prosecution of perpetrators of criminal offences and the enforcement of penalties. The scope of the proposed provisions is determined by reference to separate laws under which authorised entities conduct proceedings or carry out activities in the above mentioned. range. This means that the powers of the entity as defined in the legal provisions of the law will be a factor determining the application of the provisions of the proposed law. This design of the proposed rules is primarily the result of the multiplicity of entities operating in the area of the subject-matter framework of Directive 2016/680. Such a solution is so universal that, in the event of the creation, liquidation or transformation of operators in this area, it will not be necessary to amend the proposed legislation. The draft law sets out the rights of persons whose personal data are processed by the competent authorities and the legal remedies available to them, as well as the way in which personal data are supervised. The functions of the supervisory authority under Directive 2016/680 will be exercised by the President of the Office for the Protection of Personal Data established under the Act of 10 May 2018 on the Protection of Personal Data. This solution is beneficial not only for economic reasons - there is no need to create a separate office, but above all it guarantees the consistency of the personal data protection system and thus greater security. In this way, the independence of the supervisory authority is also ensured as an essential part of the system as a whole. On the other hand, the Directive provides for the possibility of introducing a different regime relating to criminal proceedings. According to Art. 18 of the Directive, as well as recitals 20 and 49 of its preamble, the national legislature may in such a way establish the protection of personal data collected in criminal proceedings in order to comply with the national procedure governing those proceedings. In recital 20 of the Directive, the EU legislature also pointed out that the Directive should not prevent Member States from defining in national criminal law procedural transactions and procedures for the processing of personal data by courts and other judicial authorities. Therefore, with regard to the courts and the prosecutor's office, separate supervisory authorities guaranteeing the independence of the courts and the prosecutor's office are laid down in the provisions amending other laws. The draft law focuses on the tasks of the President of the Office arising from the specificities of the processing of data for the purpose of preventing crime, conducting investigations, detecting and prosecuting perpetrators of criminal offences and executing penalties. They shall complement the tasks of the President as laid down in the Personal Data Protection Act. The President of the Office will have the possibility to carry out checks, to process personal data, using, as a rule, the procedures regulated by the Personal Data Protection Act. This solution constitutes an additional guarantee element, in the maximum possible approach to both systems for the protection of personal data, but also taking into account their specific characteristics. The President of the Office, of his own motion or at the request of the person concerned, shall be able, by means of an administrative decision, to order the controller or the processor to restore his or her lawful status. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The rights of persons set out in Chapter 4 of the draft law, i.e. the right of access to personal data - art. 23 that right has been defined as making available or transferring to the applicant a copy of the personal data or drawn up in an accessible form an extract from those data; the right to supplement, update or rectify personal data where those data are incomplete, outdated or false - Article. 24; the right to delete personal data where such data have been collected or processed in breach of the provisions of this Law - also Article. 24. Where it is found that the data are incomplete, outdated or incorrect, the controller shall ex officio delete the data. According to the draft article. 25 The controller will be obliged without undue delay to temporarily restrict the processing of personal data if: the data subject disputes the correctness of personal data and their correctness or irregularities cannot be established; and personal data are not deleted, must be... Excuse me: personal data that are being deleted must be retained for evidence. The proposed rules give the data subject the opportunity to request information concerning, inter alia, the purposes for which personal data, sources of data are to be used today, the right to lodge a complaint with the President of the Office or another supervisory authority on the basis of separate provisions, in the event of a breach of the rights of the person as a result of the processing of his or her personal data and the contact details of the President of the Office or of another supervisory authority. The controller will also provide information on the name, location and contact details of the controller and, if necessary, on the contact details of the data protection officer for the purpose of implementing the above mentioned. the rights of persons. A person whose personal data has been processed unlawfully shall be entitled to lodge a complaint with the President of the Office for the Protection of Personal Data within 30 days from the date of notification of the breach or receipt of information from the controller. On the other hand, the draft assumes that information will not be provided if it would result in: disclosure of information obtained as a result of operational and exploratory activities, making it difficult or impossible to identify, detect or combat criminal offences or prevent them, hindering the conduct of criminal proceedings, criminal enforcement, criminal tax proceedings or in cases of criminal offences or tax offences. Information will also not be provided in situations of threat to life, human health or security or public order, threats to national security, including defence or security, and economic grounds for the functioning of the State, as well as significant violations of personal property of other persons. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The introduction of the possibility for the administrator not to provide information is based on the grounds set out in Directive 2016/680 as set out in Article. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 and Article 3 Article 15 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1. Your Marshal, shall I finish, or may I continue?

There are two things here that are controversial. or at least curiosity, but with a rather cautious attitude, maybe negative. Of course, it's the D.A.'s office and the Secret Service. As regards the prosecutor's office, please, as a factor determining the acceptance of the exemption referred to in Article. 3 point 1 of the draft law should indicate the conceptual scope of the definition of the data set. In the light of doctrine and case law, the data set as defined in Article. Point 6 of the Directive should meet the following criteria. First of all, it must be a set of personal data. Secondly, personal data in this set must be arranged in such a way that the requested information can be found without the need to search the entire set. Third, data must be structured according to at least two specific criteria, and these criteria should relate to individuals. So the set is to be arranged in such a way that personal data can be searched without the need to search the entire set. This means that the evidence collected and documented in case files and activities does not create a set of data, i.e. they do not exhaust these criteria, this definition. They do not constitute a uniform set or can not be searched according to specific criteria for personal data contained in the evidence. The criteria according to which the file is organised apply not to personal data, but to the order in which the evidence or proceedings concerning normally separate criminal offences are carried out. This is as short as possible to answer to the committee's discussion on this exclusion of prosecutors' files. As far as the special services are concerned, the application of the proposed law has indeed excluded the area of national security, including from an institutional perspective special services, i.e. Internal Security Agency, Intelligence Agency, Military Counterintelligence Service, Military Intelligence Service and Central Anti-corruption Office. It should be stressed that Directive 2016/80, which is currently being implemented in this draft law, permits such a solution. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 2 (2) 3 lit. a indicates that it does not apply to the processing of personal data within an activity not covered by Union law. In this context, it should be recalled that the Treaty on European Union in Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. The second sentence 3 states that, in particular, national security remains the exclusive responsibility of each Member State. At the same time, the provision of the Article. 2 (2) 3 lit. and the Directives should be read taking into account recital 14, which clarifies the scope of the Directive. According to recital 14, the provisions of the Directive should not apply to the processing of personal data in the course of activities outside the scope of Union law. At the same time, the concept of activities outside the scope of Union law is developed by identifying three areas which include: firstly, national security activities, secondly, agency or national security entities, thirdly, the processing of personal data by Member States during activities which fall within the scope of Title V of Chapter 2 of the Treaty on European Union. It is important to note that the European legislator lists separately, as you can see, national security activities and the activities of national security agencies or bodies. This is a substantive and formal justification for the applicability and appropriateness of this solution. But, of course, we will certainly still talk about this legitimacy at the committee or subcommittee meeting, because it is worth examining and discussing things in detail, and if there are doubts, then explain them. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! That's all I'd like to say when it comes to answering these questions. Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Health Janusz Cieszyński will present the justification for the bill. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u559	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Good evening. I am very pleased to present on behalf of the government the justification for the bill on e-direction and waiting lists. This is another eHealth project that I have the pleasure of presenting to the High House. This project contains three key elements. The first is the introduction of regulations on the issue and implementation of electronic referrals. Electronic targeting is the functionality on platform P1, on the platform, which is the largest project currently funded by EU funds, from digital projects. This is a project that we have inherited, and we are now implementing its second phase, after eight years of failure to achieve its goals. As part of this project, we provide further elements of the eHealth ecosystem, and after the successful piloting of the electronic prescription, we have now moved to the next element, in line with the project plan, namely the electronic referral. Electronic referral is a document that supports the transfer process from one doctor, usually the primary healthcare doctor, to the other, to hospital treatment, to a specialist clinic. Or it is also about benefits - and here is an exception - in which there is radiation and which each time require the consent of a doctor, even in the case of those financed by private funds of a patient. This is, for example. C.T. scan. Electronic referrals are a product that we started in mid-October. The first phase of piloting is currently underway. As part of this first phase, we are working on integration between our system and doctors' office systems. This primarily serves to ensure that, before piloting starts in such a sensitive sector as the health system, it is possible to ensure that this pilotage is in principle functional from the first days, and that the errors that will occur as part of the pilotage will not have a fundamental character that could undermine confidence in the entire eHealth ecosystem. As part of this regulation, we indicate among others. the types of benefits or we are giving a delegation to a regulation indicating the types of benefits that will be covered by an electronic referral. We also point to emergency procedures and describe a number of technical issues related to the implementation of e-direction. Let me tell you a little bit. E-direction, i.e. The document in electronic form does not require, first of all, that it is a patient, a person who is a carrier of information. He was a carrier of information in a common sense, because today the paper referral needs to be carried out, the patient himself does so, and the electronic referral at the time of recording it in the system is available to every realizer in Poland. All you have to do is call, give a reference number or give a PESEL and a 4-digit security code and, on the basis of this, the realist can save us for the appropriate benefit. We also hope that the introduction of electronic referrals will help shorten the queues, because we know that there is a phenomenon that patients sign up for more than one specialist advice from a doctor of the same specialty. And by electronic referral at the moment when such a referral is issued, the doctor of a particular specialty will no longer be able to issue a second one, which is completely uncontrollable in a situation where the system is purely paper. And this is the first most important change. The second amendment concerns provisions related to waiting lists, i.e. colloquially speaking queues. The most important change is the considerable flexibility in the management of waiting lists. And this is a practical example. What happens today when the patient cancels his appointment? Under the current rules, a patient should be invited to replace him, who has another visit, e.g. Half an hour later, and this patient and all other patients should be informed that the deadline has shifted, because in fact the whole list should be moved by one eye, inform, otherwise, in writing. As you all certainly know, this is a completely dead recipe. Fortunately, because it would be a great waste of resources. We're proposing a new solution. Within this solution, it will be possible to manage places on waiting lists flexibly by indicating by the healthcare provider, by the clinic, the deadline by the hospital to the patient who cancelled the visit, to any person who has a deadline of between 7 and 30 days from the original one. Why 7 to 30 days? It's a practical application. If we could convince... If we were to allow this to be done to the full extent, in an extreme case we could offer a visit to someone who had it planned for a day later, but as you know, a day later most of us have plans and such a change would not be comfortable at all. At the same time, if the end period was extended indefinitely, there could be abuse. From 7 to 30 days, on the one hand, it offers a practical benefit and on the other hand it prevents abuse. In addition, we implement the request of patient communities, giving them the opportunity to report on the nearest delivery date in daily cycles. This is the answer to the postulates that emerged after the National Health Fund provided the portal with time limits for treatment. On this portal you can, just remind, check when and where you can get a given type of benefit in the near future. This is so important that very often the diversity in availability is very large even in the same county or voivodship, and many patients would be willing to even overcome a certain distance, so that the waiting time is shorter. And this leads to a more efficient management of the resources that we have in the health system, and access to this information only makes sense when they are up to date. Today we know from patients that this update every week, and sometimes even less so, while keeping the gate for those smaller healthcare providers who have some technical problems, and giving them the opportunity with the agreement of the director of the application department still weekly. This could be about, for example. clinics that give advice once a week. It wouldn't make any sense for them to inform each day about the current deadline, because it's just going on. A week doesn't change. In addition, we ask the healthcare providers to provide the National Health Fund with delivery schedules. Today only waiting lists are sent to the National Health Fund, i.e. such a queue, but only in respect of the selected group of benefits, so-called. first-timers, i.e. the first consultation, which occurs only once in the case of repeated visits therapy. We want to know the full timetables for all benefits, including those with a treatment plan, and we expect the providers to report this information to us. This is not a great additional obligation, because any provider who provides benefits according to the schedule that has the schedule of visits already has this information. So that there is no need for additional reporting. And the third thing that is important in terms of introducing health solutions is support for pharmacists. Ladies and gentlemen, 1 January is an obligation for every pharmacy in Poland, firstly, to implement electronic prescriptions and secondly, which at this stage is much more important, to generate an electronic document for the implementation of the prescription. What does that mean? That means we eliminate a million paper taxa recipes overnight. About as many as there are every day in Poland. The electronic prescription document is stored in the system and is immediately visible to the patient's account at the patient's address. gov.pl and does not require any additional reporting from the pharmacist. Moreover, the checks of the National Health Fund will no longer have to visit the pharmacy, but will be able to analyse the implementation documents recorded in the system and only on this basis assess whether there has been any irregularities. It saves time for the pharmacist and the fund. So that following the entry into force of this obligation, after consulting the pharmacists' environment, we decided to take unprecedented action. It fits into a broader context because this year for the first time in the history of Polish eHealth, support instruments financed from the state budget were allocated twice as much as European funding for the implementation of the P1 project. So far, we have often been accused of financing very generously the construction of central systems, systems of the Minister of Health, and we do not have the resources to support those who are their users, namely doctors and pharmacists. And this year, the amount of PLN 65 million will be paid to doctors and pharmacists after the law enters into force, which is twice what we have allocated to the development of platform P1. This support for pharmacists will allow any pharmacist to deduct from the tax base the cost acquired in 2018, we are talking about the whole year, software, equipment and accessories, so we are talking about computers, pharmacy software, code reader, all that is needed to equip a pharmacy position when it comes to computer science. This is the form we agreed with the Chief Pharmacy Chamber. It has met with a very good reception of the environment and we can see that this systematically increases the number of certificates of access to our system. The deadline is 1 January or even 31 December, and the rate of growth is so good that we are sure that even without a panic attack on the last day or week we will be able to achieve our goal of connecting about 15 thousand. pharmacies for this system by the end of this year. These are the three most important points: e-locating, better queue management and support for the pharmacist. And this is another project that supports the implementation of eHealth systems, which is a priority of the government of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki. Thank you very much.

Studies by private market operators show that this may reduce the number of missed visits by up to 50%. The possibility of flexible management of late dates should also be assessed positively. The clinic can offer them to any patient whose appointment is 7 to 30 days later. Thanks to this solution, the patient has a real benefit from faster access to the benefit and at the same time the occurrence of undesirable events that could occur in the absence of this 30-day upper limit is limited. A dead obligation to inform in writing about the change of the deadline is also being removed. The provider may use any means of communication for this purpose. More information will also be sent to the NFZ, which will thus be able to analyse the timetable for granting all benefits and not just the benefits provided to the patient for the first time. As expected by patient organisations, this will also improve the quality of data on the nearest free date. ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u575	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you very much for these questions, because they can be said how in the lens focus the questions we receive from patients, from doctors, from pharmacists, from all these stakeholders in the health care system. Firstly, I would like to address the issue of measures, because it has been addressed by many parties. Well, ladies and gentlemen, this project provides for measures for pharmacists - I admit - in such an extraordinary manner, because we are at the end of 2018 and they concern this year. This is due to the fact that in the course of the implementation of the electronic prescription it turned out that about 1/3 pharmacists operate on computers equipped with outdated operating systems, the use of which regardless of the functioning of the electronic prescription is simply a threat to the data of patients that are stored on this equipment. We had a choice: just to spell reality and say that these are the responsibilities, the pharmacists will adapt or apply positive solutions - and so we did - and hence the decision to support pharmacists. Earlier this year, in September, the High Chamber adopted a bill proceded by the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy for the introduction of e-releases. This law transferred PLN 50 million to IT this year, which was paid to family doctors. That was the amount that went down in connection with the electronic layoffs, because family doctors are doing 80%. electronic layoffs, hence the logic of this solution. As regards other activities, local government hospitals have very large resources from regional operational programmes. Whether these funds are spent effectively, it is not for me to judge, but certainly a positive aspect of these projects is that many hospitals have received money for equipment, and this equipment, whether it is really used for these projects, will be able to be used for e-releases, e-recipes, e-directions, electronic medical records and all other e-solutions. With regard to hospitals for which the founding authority is the Minister of Health, we carry out this from the funds of the Operational Programme (Polska Cyfrowa), the e-service project. As part of this project, we will be computerising the largest hospitals, research institutes, which belong to the Minister of Health, so that we can say that hospitalization is also largely equipped or has the opportunity to apply for such measures. We are left with specialist offices, but in this case we need to consider the model of support. These are often clinics that they take, for example. 1 day a week. Here the simple question arises as to whether it is really appropriate to invest public funds in the purchase of equipment and software for such entities that carry out such benefits, e.g. Once weekly. In the opinion of the Minister for Health This is the group to which such measures should come in the last place, therefore, in order not to leave the providers alone to leave such a gap, next year we will make available an application which, in the form of a web browser available from each web browser, will make it possible to issue electronic prescriptions, issue referrals and use this eHealth ecosystem. This is intended primarily to ensure that everyone can use a secure and free application just as the Social Insurance Institution did. Here is the issue that you mentioned, namely the issue of effectiveness of implementation, how to prepare for it. Because it's not just a technological challenge, but it's also very much an organisational challenge. In my opinion, the Social Insurance Institution carried out this task very well, carried out over 35 thousand throughout Poland. training, they were carried out not only in group form, but also in the most expected form, i.e. the so-called. post training. Any doctor could have asked a trainer at the plant to come in and on his equipment, on his software, showed how to use electronic layoffs. What are the effects? The results are that in those voivodships where doctors really wanted to use it... It can be said that Warsaw, where there are more entities, more private entities, where it is easier. No. Which voivodship is the leader? West Pomerania, especially Koszalin. Excellent score. We see that there is no division into big cities, small cities. It can be seen that every region, if it wants to, can as much as possible lead to a very high level in this region when it comes to issuing these documents in electronic form. So that we are going to take advantage of the best practices that the Social Insurance Institution has shown us, and similar training we are going to implement for doctors. We are already carrying out a very large cycle of training for pharmacists, including station training for the smallest pharmacies, where there is a very practical obstacle: the pharmacist accepts himself, works alone, so when we organize training during working hours, he is not on this training because he is at work. Therefore, we use all these ways to reach the widest possible number of doctors and pharmacists, and if we can convince these groups, it is possible in our view to implement this. We are now moving on to the last group - but in fact this group should be the first - that is, patients. The professor asked about the printout. You asked me what a patient who doesn't use IT tools will do. Firstly, I have excellent news: Poles are very happy to use these IT tools, as said by the Member who presented the position of Law and Justice. More than half of the tax returns in Poland, ladies and gentlemen, those personal ones, not those who submit on our behalf to the accountant, are submitted in electronic form, more than half of the applications for 500+, 300+ - also in electronic form. From our experience, electronicisation does not only reach those younger ones, those who are more intuitively associated with such solutions, because more than half of the patients in the e-recipes pilot were patients over the age of 60. At the same time, we do not want to force anyone to participate in this digitalization. And that's what a printout is for. Any patient who wants a printout that looks like this referral we know today will be able to get it. Why? Because we want to show that this process will not be so different at first. Until everyone has an online patient account, we do not want to force them to use digital solutions. However, there is a fundamental difference, because the referral is no longer on paper, but it is in the system, and the paper is only information, it is only an image of what is in this system. I move on to the last doubts, namely the questions of Mrs Chybicka, whether the introduction of e-direction will prevent the choice of provider. Absolutely not, e-direction will be implemented as it is today. The only thing we do in this project is that we want to provide both the fund and patients with more information about where they can access the category of benefits more quickly. We still leave 100 percent freedom so that the patient can decide which selection criteria he will follow, deciding where he wants to pursue. On the other hand, we eliminate the pathological phenomenon, which was that, fearing that the waiting time is too long, the patient registered at the same time for several tips of the same type, blocking the place on the list for other patients. I think this is where I managed to answer all the questions. Thank you very much for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-11-22-sejm-72-2.u581	Ladies and gentlemen! As a Member of Pomerania Land, which also represents the Malbork and Tczewski District, I have tabled, at the request of the inhabitants, an amendment to the national budget for 2019, on which we are about to begin to process, asking for money to renovate the national road no. 22 from Malbork to Czarlin. The inhabitants of Malbork and Tczewski districts have been demanding for years to repair this road, which is a transit link from Kaliningrad through Poland to Berlin. It is in very bad condition, and on several kilometers there is not even asphalt surface, there is only used pavement surface. This is one of the last sections of the paved national road in Poland. The condition of this surface is not only burdensome for drivers, but especially for residents of houses along the mentioned paved road. Cars generate very big noise, let alone trucks. Apart from the most important - the entry from the pavement of the asphalt road suddenly on the pavement is simply dangerous, because it threatens to slip the car. Ladies and gentlemen! The inhabitants of Malbork and Tczewski districts deserve long-awaited renovation of this national road No 22. I appeal to Members, mainly PiS, Pomorskie, because it is you who have a majority in the Sejm, so you are voting out what you want, to support this amendment to my budget regarding the use of money for the renovation of this road. Thanks to this, it will be possible to start repairing this route next year. Thank you very much.
